| RR-T17.7-20261024 | T17.7 | Hermes live capture zero-page defect — root cause CONFIRMED and fixed: `hookStats` counts hook FIRES, not recorded events (handlers increment the counter before `_ensure_plugin()` returns None while tracing is off), so 258 post / 235 completed with pages=0 meant the tracer did not exist yet, not that it dropped events; two conditions produced it (tracing enabled after the calls; installed plugin older than the repo). Fixes: `tool`/`tool_name` fallback, `extract_page_body()` for plain-string results, state on `sys._ctxvm_tracer_state` (survives re-import), flush-all instead of per-session lookup, idempotent `finish()` (no duplicated turn.render/call.meta on repeated flush), `skipped_untraced` counter + richer `/ctxvm-trace status` (version / register_calls / per-tracer events+pages). 21/21 tests; isolated e2e through the installed plugin path: tracing-on-first → 3 pages / 0 skipped, tracing-late → 1 page / 5 skipped (reproduces the failure and names it); plugin.yaml → 0.2.0; packages/hermes/BUG_REPORT.md rewritten with confirmed diagnosis + deployment and operating rules. run-ci ALL GATES GREEN. |

| RR-W19DESIGN-20261105 | W19 | TIER 2'S BOUNDARY, WRITTEN BEFORE ITS CODE — `design/W19-tier2-interventions.md` (design note only; implementation owner-gated on model spend). Two tiers: tier 1 is byte-identical over recorded bytes, tier 2 runs a model with one thing changed, and the rule is absolute — a tier-2 artifact NEVER enters the deterministic boundary (not `corpus/`, not a shared checksums file, not a shared claim), because a counterfactual copied into `corpus/` makes the next determinism check certify a non-deterministic object. RUN RECORD is a sidecar `<run>.intervention.json` (not a tenth event): parent trace + digest, intervention kind/target/replacement, sampling, model identity, `nonDeterministic: true` as a literal; four fields non-optional because without them a counterfactual is not reproducible. THREE PREREGISTERED EXPERIMENTS with arms, metric, materiality and KILL conditions: E1 hint injection with the FACE-Eval channel comparison (A1 tool return vs A2 user message) and the joint table (answer moved / nothing said); E2 page ablation against the touch ratio, which is the only operational test of what a fault MEANS — `needed` := removing the page changes the answer, and tier 1's 99.3% cannot test that; E3 eviction of an early wrong premise with an ORACLE arm, because without oracle the result cannot separate 'eviction helped' from 'the premise was harmless'. THREATS NAMED BEFORE THE FIRST CALL: the intervention is visible (our own evaluation-awareness problem), provider drift under a pinned name, sampling noise at small n, page granularity is coarse by choice, and an incomplete capture poisons every 'no change' (W17 coverage is the mitigation). REFUSALS: never write into corpus/, never reuse a tier-1 filename, never re-run a validator to make a run pass, never average across temperatures, never report n=1 as a result. ACCEPTANCE TESTS for any implementation: provenance checker with a negative per field and for a parent-digest mismatch; a test that `corpus/` stays the frozen set; `monitor_replay --check` unchanged by any tier-2 run; `nonDeterministic: false` is a hard error. NOT CLAIMED: no safety evaluation, no transparency, no saving |
| RR-TSORDER-20261105 | TS-ORDER | THE TEST ASSERTED AN INVARIANT THE WIRE DOES NOT HAVE, AND THE 11-03 FIX WAS WRONG THE SAME WAY — the gate went red on a docs-only commit (`7efd683`, W19) in `packages/node/test/tracer.test.ts:217` on 'tail snapshots are time-monotonic': same values, different order (675,675,675,675,676,676,676,676 vs 675,675,676,676,675,675,676,676). ROOT CAUSE: `finish()` emits the tail GROUPED BY CLASS — every turn.render 0..N, then every call.meta — and both carry the stamp cached at that turn's render, so the tail sequence is [s0..sN, s0..sN] and is monotonic ONLY when every stamp is equal, i.e. when the run fits in one millisecond. On 11-05 it spanned 675->676 ms. The grouping is deliberate (tail re-derived per flush for D9 byte-identity; order canonical by class and turn). `8d0be1d` replaced 'whole stream monotonic' with 'tail monotonic' — both true only under clock luck, which is why the 11-03 flake never went away, it went quiet. FIX asserts the four things the code has: live prefix non-decreasing, tail grouped by class (renders before metas), turn stamps ascending, and each call.meta carrying EXACTLY its render's stamp (the snapshot law that protects D9). The same wrong assertion existed in `packages/hermes/test/test_plugin.py` (`sorted(stamps) == stamps` over the whole stream, with an identical grouped tail) and is fixed in the same card so the lanes cannot disagree. RED-BEFORE: giving call.meta a fresh `stamp()` instead of `now` turns 2 tests red; restore -> 21 pass. PROCESS FAILURE RECORDED: piping the gate into `tail -2` before `&& git commit` returns TAIL's status, so the gate failed and the commit ran anyway — standing correction is to run it unpiped and capture `$?` before staging. No event reordered, no field moved, no schema touched; consumers that sort a tail by ts would see the sawtooth and none does today. node 21, hermes 117, ALL GATES GREEN (unpiped, exit 0) |
| RR-W20DESIGN-20261105 | W20 | FOUR EXTENSIONS PRICED BEFORE THEY ARE BUILT — `design/W20-trace-surface-extensions.md`, design only, each item a FIELD, a SIDECAR, or a refusal to proceed, because RFC-0.1 is additive and a new event type would need v0.2. (1) REFUSALS + policy version: nothing records a decline today (a refusal is a turn with no declares); two shapes priced — an `outcome` field on turn.render cached and re-derived in the tail (W13 pattern) versus a `<file>.outcomes.json` sidecar — with the honest difficulty named: turn.render is stamped at RENDER and a refusal arrives AFTER the response, so a mid-session flush omits it and a crash before flush loses it. (2) PROVENANCE: `origin` (user/tool/retrieval/model/system), `trust` (trusted/untrusted/unknown, ASSERTED never inferred), optional `readers` — the data-flow half a CaMeL-style layer needs, with enforcement staying in the harness; absence must never read as trusted. (3) PAGING AS AN ATTACK SURFACE: safety-instruction eviction, thrash induction, and RE-FETCH LAUNDERING (an evicted attacker page returns with a fresh tool_recall fault, indistinguishable in the stream from a legitimate recall); three-arm tier-2 experiment proposed, not run; blocked on (2) for 'was this page important'. (4) TAMPER EVIDENCE: per-drop checksums and the W17 traceDigest are integrity against accidental change by a party WITHOUT write access — not evidence against a party that can rewrite both trace and checksum, which is the same party in a realistic audit; four options priced (hash chain = a field per event + v0.2; signed Merkle root = best ratio, recommended; transparency log and TEE attestation out of scope). DECISION TABLE: field-vs-sidecar, harness-vs-configured trust, fail-closed or not, signing in scope or not — all owner calls, none implemented. ACCEPTANCE TESTS if built: five touchpoints per field, ABSENCE IS UNKNOWN never a flattering default, a refusal without policyVersion is rejected, a verifier fails closed on a missing signature, and no test may assert that a field makes a run safer |
| RR-W21-20261105 | W21 | A REUSED TRACE NAME CAN NO LONGER REPLACE ANOTHER SESSION'S BYTES — the lesson from RR-LIVE-20261104 (a post-restart capture overwrote the 13:0x evidence under identical filenames) is now a rule with a test per lane. Before a flush writes anything it reads `<file>.meta.json`: a DIFFERENT session id gets `<file>-<sha256(sessionID)[:8]>` with the same suffix on the meta and coverage sidecars; an absent, unreadable or same-session sidecar keeps `<file>`, so a re-flush stays idempotent and D9 byte-identity is untouched. Resolved PER FLUSH from the sidecar, never cached per process, because the meta file is the authority for ownership. The retention `keep` guard and the W17 manifest's `traceFile` now follow the RESOLVED name — with a suffix, passing the base name would leave the just-written file unprotected by the prune guard. THREE LANES: hermes `_collision_safe_file` (used by `_write_trace` and `_flush_traces`, so partials share the resolved name), opencode `collisionSafeFile` in `flushTraces` (mid-session trigger and cleanup share one path), ai-sdk `collisionSafeName` in `writeCapture` (the manifest is built with the resolved name). TESTS 119 hermes / 62 opencode / 46 ai-sdk: the other session's sentinel bytes are byte-identical afterwards, the suffixed triple exists, the manifest's traceFile matches, and the hermes test drives the W17 checker over the suffixed pair; a second hermes test flushes the same session twice and asserts no suffix ever appears. Gate log line corrected to the true counts (the log had drifted; it is a log, not an assertion). NOT CLAIMED: a session still overwrites itself (idempotency by design), 8-hex collisions are negligible not impossible, naming is neither redaction nor tamper evidence, and no wire byte changed |

| RR-IDENTITY-20261106 | PLUGIN | THE DOCUMENTED REMEDY FOR `provider: unknown` WAS DEAD, AND THE LOG SAID SO ON EVERY LOAD — `WARNING Rejected config path 'model' from plugin ctxvm-tracer` was read as noise since 0.4.1. ROOT CAUSE, verified against the host: `ctx.get_config` refuses any plugin-relative key whose FIRST segment is reserved (`hermes_cli/plugins_state.py:17` = model/plugins/security/settings); `_plugin_relative_segments('model')` raises while `provider` and `identity.model` pass. The identity reader asked for `model` and `provider` inside ONE try, so the raise on `model` aborted the loop before `provider` was ever read and the fail-open handler swallowed it — meaning `settings.model` was unreachable AND the `settings.provider` line the reports have told the owner to write never executed. THE SUITE STAYED GREEN because its fake get_config mapped bare keys and did not enforce the reserved-root rule: a permissive fake hid a real refusal. FIX (0.10.1): read the non-reserved `identity` mapping first (`settings.identity.model` / `.identity.provider`), then each bare key in its OWN try, so a refused root costs one field; bare `settings.provider` still works because `provider` is not reserved; absence is still absence. LIVE CHECK on the INSTALLED copy with a stub enforcing the host rule: identity mapping -> both fields; refused model root -> provider still read. ALSO FIXED: `plugin.yaml` `provides_hooks` declared SIX while the plugin registers SEVEN — `post_api_request`, the hook carrying the W8 receipt, was missing from the manifest; now seven. hermes 171 tests (the new ReservedRootCtx stub asserts both cases). NOT PROVEN: a live settings read end to end (needs a restart with 0.10.1 and a real key); no provider is invented, old traces stay `unknown` |
| RR-IDENTITY2-20261106 | PLUGIN | THE NOISE ITSELF, REMOVED — 0.10.1 fixed the identity read but still probed the bare `model` key in its own try, so the host kept logging `Rejected config path 'model'` on every load. A call that can only fail is noise, and that noise is what hid the original defect for four releases: 0.10.2 drops the bare-model probe entirely (model comes from the `identity` mapping or not at all; bare `provider` still read because it is not reserved). TWO TEST FAKES WERE THE REASON IT HID: both mapped bare `model`/`provider` keys and never enforced the host's reserved-root rule, so the suite certified a call the host refuses; both now mirror the host (raise on model/plugins/security/settings). hermes 171 tests; installed copy synced to 0.10.2 |

| RR-HEADROOM-20261107 | CODEC-EMITTER | AN OVER-BUDGET CAPTURE WAS UNREADABLE, AND THE NAIVE ARM IS OVER BUDGET BY DESIGN — found by reading back the orchestrator's own trace: `ctxvm stream` refused a live file with `invalid value: integer -225267, expected u64` on a `turn.render` whose resident set was 425,267 against a 200,000 budget. `headroom` is `Option<u64>` in the Rust model, and BOTH emitters computed `budgetTokens - residentTokens` unconditionally, so the strict codec rejected the event and with it the whole file (stream/replay/profile/diff all refuse it, not just the line). THE RUN CONSEQUENCE: the naive arm's pressure comes from letting the context grow past the declared budget, so every naive capture would have carried a negative headroom and been unreadable — the failure would have appeared at analysis time, after the spend. FIX: omit the key when the resident set exceeds the budget — not a clamp to 0 (which reads as 'full' and is false at +225k), not a signed widening (the v0 field is sealed); a reader computes the overrun from `budgetTokens - residentTokens`. Patched in `packages/hermes/src/plugin.py` and `packages/node/src/tracer.ts` (the TS tracer is the source for the opencode and ai-sdk lanes, so all three inherit it). TESTS: python 171->172, node 21->22 (5,000-token page against a 1,000 budget omits the key; against 9,000 it carries 4000). NOT CHANGED: the wire (headroom stays optional and unsigned), the one bad capture (append-only; rewriting it would be forgery), and the engine's vBudget (unrelated field) |
| RR-STOPSEM-20261107 | PLUGIN-STOP | ONE SESSION'S STOP SILENCED A WHOLE GATEWAY — the W8 orchestrator lost capture after a `/ctxvm-trace stop` at 13:29:20 while the process env still carried CTXVM_TRACE_DIR=/tmp/w8/traces and CTXVM_TRACE_NAME=w8. Mechanism: `_active_trace_dir()` treats `state["trace_dir"] == ""` as 'explicitly stopped in this process; do not fall back to env', and stop set exactly that sentinel — but the state is PROCESS-WIDE and a gateway serves every session, delegated work included, so one operator action disabled a deployment. Two misleading signals in the same minute: '0 traces written' (which reads as 'nothing captured', but meant 'nothing pending') and silence that looks like an idle system. FIX (0.10.3): capture configured by the deployment's ENVIRONMENT is not one session's preference — stop flushes and then falls back to the env, leaving the process armed and saying so ('STAYS ARMED … this process serves many sessions — use /ctxvm-trace stop --hard to silence it'); `stop --hard` keeps the old behaviour as an explicit escape hatch, and a capture STARTED by a slash command is still stopped outright because the command, not the env, owns it. Message changed to 'nothing was pending to flush … this does not mean no events were captured', with a test pinning that '0 traces written' is gone. TESTS: python 172->173 (env-set stop stays armed then --hard disables; command-started stop still disables). RULE FOR THE RECORD: a gateway serving a measurement must not let a session-level command change deployment-level capture state; any future command mutating process-wide state needs a per-session or --hard boundary |

Captures verify (coverage_check + byte-identical double replay) and pass the DQ scan. The grid did not start; three ways forward are named in the report |

PASS, SHARPLY BOUNDED: the tool-args channel implements Part 1 literally — instruction-like strings ride single-line with every control escaped, and the stub-prefix forgery case is capped at exactly prefix + 120 + # + 8 hex + close, digest-stamped — with ONE seam recorded: a crafted pad placed a unicode-0001 escape starting at canonical index 115 so the 120-cut preserved 5 of 6 escape chars, shipping an orphan fragment before the digest (nothing re-parses ref args today). FAIL EVERYWHERE DOWNSTREAM: fileRef/summaryRef/imageRef and the tracer's pre-built ref override are raw and uncapped — a 48,000-char path survives whole through wire and core replay (exit 0); a multi-line instruction path flows raw; DEL and RTL-override bytes ride the line; the ref-grammar parse is confusable via the tool NAME zone. The core replays authored stubs VERBATIM (stub byte-identity end-to-end on a mechanically mutated corpus-m1 trace), and export's own DQ block reports replayDeterminism true and sourceFidelity true on that forged trace — the toolchain guarantees determinism and identity, NOT benign-ness, exactly the boundary the draft's Part 1 closes. THE MODEL-FACING BRIEFS FAIL THE OUTSIDE-FIELD CLAUSE MECHANICALLY IN BOTH MIRRORS: a forged markdown heading at column 0, twice per brief (once from the raw multi-line ref inside the slot bullet interpolation, once from the unresolved-marker fallback whose own bracket splits across the ref's newlines); the stub body re-enters with a 2-space indent that still constitutes a heading for any consumer that md-parses (indentedHeadingForged true both lanes); the 4,800-character payload passes verbatim; ANSI escape bytes survive into model-facing text. Verdict: PARTIAL PASS — the identity mint already does what the draft asks and is worth citing; the mend belongs to the brief mirrors and the raw scheme refs (owner-placed, report-only: treat refs as data by single-line-izing or routing them through the same canonicalize-and-cap mint, generalize the per-scheme limit, and use md-safe line-prefix normalization at the stub display boundary) |

PER-CHUNK SHAPE: pre-flight with zero spend (`--plan`, git commit + clean tree, model.provider must print nous under BOTH profiles, plugin 0.10.3, running ledger total vs the 25 USD cap); one chunk; per-cell verification of the capture triple (`.jsonl` + `.meta.json` + `.coverage.json`, coverage_check exit 0, `ctxvm replay` exit 0, ledger row with receipt and a USD that matches the pinned price row, checker verdict, no stray partial); a chunk report `RR-W8CHUNK-<date>-<n>.md`; one LOG row; explicit staged commit; then STOP — the executor must not roll into the next chunk because there is time. CELL B NOT RUN, on the hard stop. Spend 1.1541 of 25, remaining 23.8459. FIXED in 0.10.5: the engine counts `select_context()` calls in `context_engine.ACTIVATIONS`, the manifest records `engine` only when that count is above zero plus `engineSelections` as the number, so `engine: null, engineSelections: 0` is a real observation. THE LIMIT NO FIELD CAN FIX: activation is a process fact, so a multiplexer serving both arms can attest neither — one gateway per arm is mandatory (plan 4c) and the runner must refuse a cell against a multi-profile gateway. SECOND FINDING, AND IT IS THE REAL ONE: cell A's 700 declares split 686 `search_files` (69.4k tokens) against 14 `read_file` (57.5k), 632 distinct refs — the task's anti-collapse premise (line shape cannot come from a content search) is FALSE in practice, so page size was never the binding constraint; the tool surface is. Two fixtures (33 parts, then 13) produced the same behaviour and outcome, `max_iterations_reached(150/150)` with no flags.json. RECOMMENDATION: build the read/write-only tool surface AND raise or remove the iteration ceiling for both arms as a recorded T0 amendment, or stop and publish the negative (3 cells, $1.15, 4.3x request growth at a 24k budget, 686/700 declares from search, ceiling hit twice) |

| RR-LESSONS-20261109 | docs | WHAT THIS CARD TAUGHT, AND WHERE IT NOW LIVES — three skill patches plus a repo card. THE ONE LINE WORTH CARRYING: every defect this card found was a MEASUREMENT OF THE WRONG OBJECT — an engine built rather than used (`engine` filled by an unconditional build at load, so it read ctxvm on a naive arm), a counter in a shared module rather than on the engine (flat sibling import resolves through sys.modules by bare name: a naive session read 1 right after a paged one), a clone rather than the original (the host deep-copies the engine per agent, so the count incremented on a copy — `engineSelections: 0` on a 14-read treatment cell that was running), a checker reading a reference rather than the artefact (hardcoded paths, PASS was a tautology), a probe rather than a cell (a one-line request never reaches the select hook, so zero selections says nothing about activation), and a naive run using `execute_code` rather than a treatment run reading (5 calls/73k vs 7 calls/448k; NTR -5.11 is the ratio between a code shortcut and a read-heavy run, not a policy effect). SKILL: `controlled-agent-experiments` gains the deepcopy/meter rule with a test shape, the probe-versus-cell distinction, the route-confound rule with the measured pair, and a CORRECTION of its own wrong instruction — `ctx.get_config` was documented as returning the serving profile's value; it reads `plugins.entries.<plugin_id>.settings.<key>` and returned null for a profile whose config says ctxvm. REPO: lane ownership and the `experiments/` publication rule stay in `handoff/LANES.md` and `AGENTS.md`, because they are conventions rather than experiment design |

| RR-W8PAIR-20261109 | W8-pair | FIRST COUNTED PAIR AND IT IS NEGATIVE — T-A, both arms `-t file`, one gateway (PID 139582, plugin 0.10.10): naive 5 calls, input 336,358 (cache-read 227,410 / write 108,933), peak 59,793, $0.0175; paged 14 calls, input 1,057,053 (cache-read 83,042 / write 973,969), peak 65,488, $0.1264. NTR = -2.14, paged 3.14x input and 7.2x dollars: selection rewrites the prefix every call, so prefix cache collapses and writes explode — the mechanism the pricing warned about, now measured, costing more than the tokens it saves. Both cells parsed-PASS with identical artefacts (`part-12.txt`, `total_long 599`); byte-format FAIL both, an observation under the parsed-equality ruling. No `execute_code`, no `terminal` in either arm — `-t file` pins the surface. ARMS ARE RUNNER-ATTRIBUTED: both sidecars read `engineSelections: 14` because a plugin in the shared plugins directory is one module for every profile — one state dict, one engine object, one counter; the differential test is unavailable on this host, and the plan now says so instead of proposing a fourth witness. Cells 2/48, spend $1.3562 of $25. Next action: do not scale; the prefix-rewrite cost has to be understood first |
| RR-LESSONS2-20261109 | docs | THE PAIR THAT COUNTED, AND WHAT IT COST TO GET THERE — four witnesses failed for one reason stated once: a plugin installed in the shared plugins directory is ONE module for every profile (first scope claims the bare name, later scopes get `__home_<digest>`), so no in-process field can distinguish an arm; both cells read `engineSelections: 14` because the control was reading the treatment's engine. The rule to apply FIRST is `read the install path, not the loader logic` — a per-arm witness is a deployment property. THE RESULT: NTR -2.14, 3.14x input, 7.2x dollars, cache-read 227k -> 83k and cache-write 109k -> 974k, both artefacts parsed-equal, so the loss is the prefix cache and not accuracy; tokens were never the right currency once a cached class exists. Also recorded: the surface was settled by one flag (`-t file`, 32 reads, 8 calls, $0.0221) after the inventory showed the code tool was never in the set, and the byte-exact gate became parsed equality so two arms cannot fail on JSON style. NEXT QUESTION IS ENGINE STRUCTURE — stable prefix across calls where residency is unchanged — one cell, not 48; packet P8 filed to the reviewer |
| RR-CACHEDESIGN-20261109 | W8 | CACHE-STABLE SELECTION: the research design accepted in four parts, and the arithmetic that gates it. Source verified at abstract level (Xu et al., arXiv:2606.17016v2, 2026-06-15/2026-08-28); its quoted figures remain body-level UNVERIFIED. THE DECISIVE NUMBER: naive's effective input cost is 13,167 price-units, so a perfectly cache-stable arm ties only below a 57,250-token per-call prefix at 14 calls (94,053 at 5, 45,405 at 20) — and the measured paged prefix was 65,488, so caching alone would still have lost. At 24,000 the same cell would cost 42% of naive. Cache stability is necessary and NOT sufficient: the engine must also shrink the prefix, and in the measured cell it grew it (+5,695 over naive) because the protected floor exceeds the 24,000 budget. Cost decomposition: paged input $0.0982 of $0.1264, and implied output 56,337 tokens against naive's 8,662 — two costs, not one. Acceptance for the one cell that tests `selectionMode: stable`: parsed-equal artefact, per-call prefix <= 24,000 at some point, prefixHash unchanged between triggers, cache-write below naive with cache-read above. If the prefix never drops below the tie, the cell is recorded as a NO-OP, not as a paging result |
| RR-BUDGET-20261109 | W8 | THE PAIR RAN AT 200,000, NOT 24,000 — both `turn.render` rows give `resident + headroom = 200,000` and `evictedTokens: 0`, so the treatment arm never had the condition applied: `engineSelections: 14` was the hook being called with 84% of its budget free. Cause: the budget came only from `CTXVM_BUDGET_TOKENS`, which the run rules forbid setting and no profile declared, so the plugin's 200000 default won in both arms — the one-factor design was satisfied on paper (`context.engine`) and defeated in practice (the budget, the other half of the treatment). A default is not a treatment. Fix 0.10.11: precedence `plugins.entries.ctxvm-tracer.settings.budgetTokens` (serving profile's config) > env > 200000, recorded as `budgetTokens` AND `budgetSource` in the coverage sidecar; the trace meta is untouched. `w8-paged` declares 24000, verified by `hermes config get … -p w8-paged` -> 24000 and `-p w8-naive` -> not set; 5 new tests, python 182. PAIR RECLASSIFIED: a no-op measurement, not a paging result. The cache arithmetic stands as arithmetic and has not been tested; cells that count 0. A treatment cell is void at turn 0 unless `budgetTokens` reads 24000 with source `settings` |

| RR-NOOP-20261109 | W8 | THE ENGINE CANNOT ACT ON A SINGLE-TURN SESSION, AT ANY BUDGET — the diagnostic cell applied the treatment for the first time (`budgetTokens 24000`, `budgetSource settings`, `engineSelections 9`) and still evicted nothing: `evictedTokens 0`, `page.evict 0`, peak 63,246 vs naive 59,793, $0.0765 vs $0.0175. Cause is granularity, not budget: every `page.declare` carries `turn: 0` (a `-z` one-shot is ONE turn, 50 results / 63,713 tokens), and the keep law protects `protect_first_n=3` head and `protect_last_n=6` tail TURNS — with one turn the protected set is the whole request, so `_plan` hits `floor_tokens >= v_budget` and returns early by design (L5). Raising or lowering vBudget changes nothing. Cell recorded NO-OP, not a paging result; cells counted stay 0/48, spend $1.4327 of $25. Two honest exits: a MULTI-TURN driver (no policy change, cheapest) or a re-preregistered MESSAGE-granular keep law (a new policy, not poolable with existing cells). Do not buy another single-turn cell; the prompt now carries a STOP banner and the plan §4e records the blocker |

| RR-PLUGIN-1012-20261110 | plugin | TRACERS PERSIST SO TURNS ACCUMULATE, WITH A RETENTION WINDOW — 0.10.12 lands the adoption lane's `flushed_sessions` guard (a flushed session's tracer was popped, which reset a 12-prompt session to one turn on every prompt) and adds what that guard was missing: `_evict_stale_tracers` releases a tracer after `CTXVM_TRACER_RETAIN_MINUTES` (default 30). Why the second half matters: keeping every tracer forever means every later flush rewrites every tracer's trace and the process grows with the session count — the leak that a long-lived gateway would pay for the correctness fix. `finish()` is idempotent (tail recomputed per call, never appended to `_events`), so retaining the tracer is safe and repeatable. Two tests pin it: a flushed session keeps its tracer inside the window, and a quiet one is dropped and removed from both sets outside it. python 182 -> 184; gate green; version guard satisfied at 0.10.12 in code and `plugin.yaml`; installed copy synced and import-verified. The running gateway (PID 112196, 11:37) still holds 0.10.11-without-eviction, so a restart is needed before the next cell |

| RR-W8B-KEYSYNC-20261110 | W8B | RE-CHECK AFTER THE KEY UPDATE: THE KEY ON DISK NEVER CHANGED. `~/.hermes/.env` still holds `sk-or-v1-179…8b9d` (73 chars, mtime 16:24) and `GET https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/key` still answers **401 User not found**; a scan of the whole `~/.hermes` tree finds exactly ONE distinct OpenRouter value, so no other store received an update; `auth.json`'s pool entry only references `env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and stores no secret. Found on the way: a profile is its own HERMES_HOME and the loader reads `<home>/.env`, so every profile copied from another carries a SNAPSHOT of the key — updating the root .env alone would still leave the run on the old key, and the failure appears as an auth error at prompt 1. The runner now syncs the root key into each arm profile's .env when they differ, and prints every file it writes. Verified: openrouter still fails fast on the 401; nvidia unaffected |

(d) is out (a third consumer would find the rule only in code comments); (a) alone is weaker than (b) (a workaround in two repos does not tell a new implementer the rule exists); (c) is out for a reason the blast-radius point misses - SEALED v0 traces exist and will not change, so every consumer of them must keep the type-dispatch rule forever, and a field would add a THIRD shape without removing either old one - VERIFY SWEEP: the reviewer's one consumer rule is VERIFIED WORD FOR WORD against BOTH schemas - sealed v0 spec/rfc-0/x-cabi.schema-v0.json gives page.declare.page -> $defs/pageRef = {id: pageId{logicalRef,contentHash}, kind, tokens} (NESTED) while page.render, page.evict, page.fault, page.pin and page.unpin -> $defs/pageId = {logicalRef, contentHash} (FLAT); RFC-0.1 keeps the same $defs and the same nine verbs (oneOf len 9 in both), so the asymmetry is inherited and unchanged; NO VERB CARRIES BOTH SHAPES, so the reviewer's falsifier "a verb that legitimately carries both shapes" is currently empty; the "dispatch on event type, never on which fields happen to be present" clause is what both in-repo consumers already do (packages/ai-sdk/src/resume-brief.ts:153-156 checks event.type !== "page.evict" BEFORE reading the flat shape; umpire spec/ledger_tools.py:85 dispatches on etype == "page.fault" - with the caveat that umpire's branch is PROFILE-SPECIFIC, "not page.fault => nested" being right only inside its two-verb profile, so the note must state the general rule) - TWO OF THE THREE UNKNOWNS RESOLVED FROM THE BOOKS: (i) schemaVersion is declared on the session.open line ONLY and is optional (spec/rfc-0.1/RFC-0.1-xCABI.md:8 "A trace MAY declare schemaVersion: \"0.1\" on its session.open line"), so the reviewer's premise "if every v0.1 line already carries schemaVersion" is FALSE and their mitigation does not apply - their conclusion still holds, on the stronger sealed-v0-data argument; (ii) the note needs a D-NUMBER, because RFC-0.1's own status line reads "Status: Applied (D4 approved 2026-10-30)" - and the landing site is already sanctioned by that document's scope line ("Scope: new files under spec/rfc-0.1/ only"), so the note can be a NEW FILE under spec/rfc-0.1/, touching neither the sealed spec/rfc-0/ nor the existing RFC text; (iii) a conformance vector is a FIXTURE, not a spec edit - the vectors live in rust/crates/ctxvm-conformance/vectors/ (events-v0.jsonl, events-v0-1.jsonl, session-clean.jsonl, session-divergent.jsonl), with the caveat that adding one changes the conformance-suite totals the CI prints and the books hardcode no counts; the MARGINAL costs are (i) every v0.1-aware consumer must be updated (the strict allowlist rejects an unknown field), (ii) two sources of identity per line, and (iii) a consistency rule between them - the ruling is unchanged, the reasoning is corrected - FALSIFIERS ADOPTED VERBATIM (any one changes the ruling): a valid v0 or v0.1 line where the type-dispatch rule gives the wrong identity; CAUSE IS THE TASK, NOT THE PROVIDER: T-A asks for lines over 100 bytes and under 10 bytes, `read_file` reports only `total_lines` and `file_size`, so the model counts and starts reproducing the file — nvidia shows `[System: Your previous response was truncated by the output…]` then `This turn did not complete` at 180 s, longcat read at 17 s and then sat silent for 283 s at a 300 s cap. The extractive control (`report the exact text of line 137`) completes in **4 s / 2 tool calls** on the same fixture, profile and model. Landed: prompt rule banning `search_files` (smoke: 21 calls → **1**), SIGTERM before SIGKILL so a timeout flushes, `--limit` for smoke checks, w8bn `max_turns` 12→6, `--expect-budget any` accepted again. Recommendation in the card: T-A v2 using only tool-reported values, before any cell — nothing measured, so nothing is invalidated |

| RR-P11CANON-20261111 | P11-canon | PACKET P11 ANSWERED (CANONICAL ORDERING) - THE RULING STANDS ((c) AS THE OWNER, WITH (a) AS A POINTER) AND THE ORDER IS TEST-FIRST; TWO OF THE REVIEWER'S UNKNOWNS RESOLVE FROM THE BOOKS; AND TWO CROSS-LANGUAGE DIVERGENCES MEASURED TODAY TRIGGER THE REVIEWER'S THIRD FALSIFIER - two passes (the reviewer's first answer plus a re-check against RR-UMRES, RR-UGATE and RR-P10ID) recorded in reports/RR-P11CANON-20261111.md; no spec, schema, vector, corpus or umpire byte changed, no model spend, nothing pushed - RULING: (c) as the owner with (a) as a short POINTER, and the ORDER matters - WRITE THE CONFORMANCE TEST FIRST, then the note can state a fact the test enforces (F9 is currently a claim nobody can falsify: no test shows a sorted-keys line and a struct-order line derive the same page table, so "a note without the test records a belief"); the test belongs to ctxvm because ctxvm's replay is what makes the acceptance claim (umpire's 00-SPEC may mention it but cannot own it); (d) is out for P10's reason - WHAT THE TEST MUST ASSERT, IN ORDER OF STRENGTH (all three should pass; a failure at any level says what broke): (1) same parsed model - one event set serialised sorted-key and struct-order parses to equal models; (2) same derived page table - replay both, equal field by field; (3) same report - ctxvm replay output including divergences and violations identical; NEVER byte-identical lines (the byte forms differ by construction, ts rides last); include a trace WITH ts and one WITHOUT (the sorted form puts ts before type - the case most likely to expose a bug) plus a numeric case; it lives in BOTH places - a vector pair with the expected report in rust/crates/ctxvm-conformance/vectors/ (so any other implementation can run it) AND a Rust unit test that parses the pair - THE NOTE'S LINE IS A COMPATIBILITY STATEMENT ONLY (verbatim): "Key order within an event is not significant to parsing or replay: events written in sorted-key order and in the emitter's field order are accepted equally; the codec remains strict about unknown fields" - not normative ordering (law 3 forbids that), the claim stays about parsing and replay, and the last clause stops the note from implying anything new is accepted - TWO UNKNOWNS RESOLVED FROM THE BOOKS: (i) ts COVERAGE - events-v0-1.jsonl carries ts on 6 LINES (events-v0.jsonl, session-clean and session-divergent carry none), so ts-bearing material exists but only ONE byte form per event and the discriminating PAIR still has to be built; (ii) a vector is a FIXTURE, not a spec edit (the vectors live outside spec/), so the pair can be added without touching a spec byte (caveat: it moves the conformance-suite totals the CI prints); plus the note's route - a D-NUMBER (RFC-0.1's status line "Applied (D4 approved 2026-10-30)") and a NEW FILE under spec/rfc-0.1/ (its scope: "new files under spec/rfc-0.1/ only"), bundling the P10 type-dispatch line and this line as TWO lines under ONE D-number - ONE PREMISE CORRECTED: "RR-IMPL2 reports no TS-to-Python byte comparison" - there IS one (RR-W7-20261029.md section 4: "umpire committed fixtures re-minted: 17 records, mismatches=0"), with the scope caveats printed beside it ("ASCII-only per umpire section 2, floats banned", labels sorted-unique) and the verdict "byte-compatible, NOT an independent implementation" (the Python side declares itself a mirror of packages/node/src/ident.ts); the assertion is well-aimed - it is exactly that ASCII-only, float-free scope which leaves their case untested - but the comparison exists; and their hash-input inference is NARROWED: umpire's content_hash and response_hash hash the content STRING directly, and only the REF's truncation digest (decide_ref -> hex8(canonical) on the canonical args) is over canonical JSON - TWO DIVERGENCES MEASURED TODAY AT ZERO SPEND, WHICH TRIGGERS THE REVIEWER'S THIRD FALSIFIER ("any digest or chain computed over serialised bytes"): umpire's Python canonical_json (json.dumps sort_keys=True, separators=(",",":"), ensure_ascii=False) versus ctxvm's TS canonicalJson (packages/node/src/ident.ts:16-21) - ascii IDENTICAL, non-ASCII values IDENTICAL, nested keys IDENTICAL, but ASTRAL-PLANE KEY NAMES DIFFER (Python sorts by Unicode code point, JS's default Array.sort by UTF-16 code unit => different canonical bytes => a different digest, silently; the VALUE side is fine, so ensure_ascii=False alone does not make them equivalent) and NUMBER TEXT FORMS DIFFER in four of six cases (1.0 -> "1.0" vs "1"; 1e16 -> "1e+16" vs "10000000000000000"; 1e-7 -> "1e-07" vs "1e-7"; an integer past 2^53 -> "12345678901234567890" vs "12345678901234567000"; 1e+30 and 0.30000000000000004 agree) - so "the canonical form of a hash input must match byte for byte across languages" is NOT SATISFIABLE AS STATED: it needs a SPECIFIED canonical form (ordering by code point, a number encoding), and since umpire's ref digest IS a digest over serialised bytes, the reviewer's third falsifier fires and the note needs a normative canonical form FOR HASH INPUTS ONLY - DUPLICATE KEYS MEASURED: the v0.1 validator ACCEPTS a line with a duplicated key ("OK: all checks passed", exit 0) and no duplicate-key check exists in the codec or either validator; serde_json, Python json and JSON.parse all take the LAST value - uniform by accident, specified nowhere - FALSIFIERS ADOPTED (any one changes the ruling): a pair of lines in the two orders whose parsed models differ; differing page tables or replay reports; any digest or chain computed over serialised bytes (TRIGGERED - see above); a consumer relying on key order to dispatch - kit: P11 packet STATUS -> answered (kept inside the packet's own 3,072 B cap by trimming its prose; the full text lives in the card and the ARCHIVE), QUESTIONS P11 row -> answered, full ARCHIVE P11 section with both answers, day-file section, 08-HANDOFF frontier 11 addendum 15, roadmap P11 tick + the measured divergences + the next-cue list (NO packet paste is pending - P1..P11 are all answered), prompt pin - the packet queue is now EMPTY of owner pastes - LOG row, PROJECT-STATE delta in its own commit |

| RR-C3FIX-20261111 | c3-fix | C3 MADE TRUE OF OUR OWN TREE - ident.ts SORTS BY UNICODE CODE POINT (THE DEFAULT SORT'S UTF-16 ORDER WAS A SILENT DIGEST DIVERGENCE), WITH A PYTHON-GENERATED CROSS-LANGUAGE VECTOR FILE, THE C3 WORDING AMENDED TO REJECT-NOT-COERCE, AND NO DIGEST MOVED ANYWHERE - the review of the landed note found the load-bearing defect: C3 was FALSE for ctxvm itself - packages/node/src/ident.ts:20 used Object.keys(obj).sort(), which compares UTF-16 code units, while Python's sort_keys=True compares code points, so for U+FF00 vs U+1D11E JS put the astral key first (first unit 0xD834) and Python last; the owner chose option (a) (fix the comparator) and the review added wording, range and trap requirements - THE FIX: a new compareCodePoints(a, b) in ident.ts compares Unicode code points (via Array.from, the string iterator's whole-code-point yield) with a prefix-first tie-break - the order Python's sort_keys uses and C3 requires; Rust's serde_json BTreeMap agrees by construction (UTF-8 byte order == code-point order), so TypeScript was the odd one out - two new tests in packages/node/test/tracer.test.ts (code-point sorting with the expected bytes taken from Python, and the vector-file test) - rust/crates/ctxvm-conformance/vectors/canonical-form-cases.jsonl: 12 cases, each with the input JSON TEXT, the expected canonical bytes and the expected digest (sha256:<8 hex>), all generated by PYTHON's canonical_json (the reference) and written ASCII-safe so the lone-surrogate case is representable - ascii, astral-key, nested-astral, prefix-key, max-safe-int, control-chars, nfc, nfd, plus the reject cases beyond-2^53, integral-float, negative-zero, lone-surrogate; the NFC and NFD cases carry DIFFERENT digests (sha256:0ca09f1d vs sha256:eed5dc86), pinning the no-normalisation rule; the file is meant to be run by umpire's Python side too, so C3 becomes a tested property on both sides - validateHashInput(value, path) added to ident.ts (exported and tested, NOT wired into the minting path, deliberately): non-integer numbers, integers outside +/-2^53-1, -0 and lone surrogates are rejected with the path in the message - C3 WORDING AMENDED (the review's points): reject-not-coerce with the duty SPLIT, because only part is visible downstream - the PRODUCER MUST NOT put a float in a hash input (JSON.parse("1.0") yields the number 1, so a consumer cannot tell an integral float from an integer, while Python keeps the float and spells it 1.0) and a consumer meeting a float value MUST reject it, never coerce it; integers must satisfy abs(n) <= 2^53-1 (a larger one is not exactly representable in an IEEE-754 double, so a consumer MUST reject it or the producer MUST carry it as a string); -0 is not admissible; strings must be valid Unicode scalar values (a lone surrogate MUST be rejected, not escaped - JS's JSON.stringify escapes lone surrogates while Python with ensure_ascii=False emits them raw and the UTF-8 encoding then fails); no normalisation is applied (NFC and NFD are different inputs; the producer owns it); and C3 now points at its committed enforcement (the vector file + the TS test that runs it) - VERIFIED THAT NO DIGEST MOVED: an independent scan BEFORE the change over 44 hashed input files (umpire's calibration corpora v2/v3, its fixtures and harness captures; ctxvm's corpus/m* and the conformance vectors) found 0 keys outside the BMP and 0 astral characters in VALUES - and since only object KEYS are sorted, the fix cannot move any existing digest (this is the precise form of the "no fixture churn" claim: it holds for ANY input whose keys are all in the BMP, whatever its values contain); after the change the full CI is ALL GATES GREEN - check-node-build OK with 16 events byte-identical (the node build's golden bytes unmoved), corpus checksums verified, SCHEMA CONFORMANCE PASS (682 checks), export round-trip OK, hub byte-identity green, TS suites 24 (the two new tests), python suites unchanged - THE EDGE CASE THE OWNER ASKED TO BE NAMED: a tool call whose arguments contain an astral-plane KEY would get a DIFFERENT logicalRef after this change (its canonical bytes change) - and the wiring deliberately NOT done: calling validateHashInput from the minting path would make a tool call whose arguments carry an integral float fail to mint a ref, a behavioural change with a blast radius beyond the note (traces would fail rather than hash differently) - owner-routed follow-up, named - ROUTED, NOT DONE HERE: (i) the approval record - the reviewer flagged that decision.md still lists "approve it, pick the D-number, and decide C3" as a pending owner decision while RR-NOTE says the note landed; I COULD NOT LOCATE decision.md in either repo (the umpire repo's design/ holds only umpire-adoption-steps.md and W9-TRANSFER.md, and the phrase appears nowhere), so the file is outside this lane's reach - what it must say: the note is RATIFIED UNDER D18 (the owner's cue "order is ok as proposed" authorised C3's ordering rule; the D-number stays - renumbering a landed note costs a citation sweep for nothing) and the three items it lists are CLOSED (note approved, D-number D18, C3 adopted and enforced); (ii) the C3 NAMING COLLISION (the W8 close-out's C3 = budget simulation vs this note's C3 = canonical-form clause) - the note now says so explicitly and the w8b lane's file is theirs to rename; (iii) the W8SIM overstatement ("No policy can save more than 5.3%" holds only for policies that keep the first three turns, the last six and the system prompt) - that sentence is in the w8b lane's card, routed not edited; (iv) C2's locator - the P11 pair and tests/key_order.rs are the owner's in-flight files and still untracked, so the note's C2 pointer becomes real when they land, while C3's pointer is ALREADY real (committed vector file + committed TS test) - kit: the code fix + two tests + the vector file + the note's amended C3 and the naming line + this card + LOG row RR-C3FIX-20261111 + day-file line + 08-HANDOFF frontier 11 addendum 23 + the roadmap's P10 entry + the prompt pin + PROJECT-STATE in its own commit; umpire's two arms run as separate profiles (naive / paged) with the plugin as one module, and their settings differ because the PROFILE carries them, resolved per invocation, not snapshotted once per process - so the analogue of this lane's recommendation is that the W8B engine's settings (budget, engine, policy) must be resolved PER SESSION OR PER PROFILE INSIDE the plugin, not captured once per gateway process: their option 2 is the analogue of the design that demonstrably separates arms in the umpire side, and option 1 (one gateway per arm) is the heavier but trivially correct version of it - WHAT A NO-CONTRAST RUN IS NOT: a run with no contrast is a HARNESS LIMIT, not evidence about cache-stable selection, paging, or the cost of a paged arm; so a W8B closure on that ground must say the free-model harness could not produce two distinguishable arms (one gateway process = one plugin instance = one settings snapshot; every sidecar attests profile: default) and make NO CLAIM IN EITHER DIRECTION - and it must not be recorded as a negative result about cache-stable selection, which is a different experiment (P8's ranking, W8's closure); two arms that both report the paged budget are exactly the failure just measured, and it must be a gate, not a reading - kit: this card + LOG row + a day-file line + the roadmap's T-A pointer; no file of the other lane's was edited, nothing pushed |

the only worktree line not byte-identical to a committed line is RR-HUBREF-20261111, whose text was REVERSED - the committed row states the in-flight hub/catalog.yaml edit rewrites the reference from "$ref: ../04-odps-dataproduct-corpus.yaml" to "$ref: 04-odps-dataproduct-corpus.yaml", which is the direction tools/build-hub.sh step 3 restores (its old = "$ref: 04-...", new = "$ref: ../04-..."), so the COMMITTED row is the correct one - WHY IT MATTERS: the LOG is the append-only ledger, and a commit that staged the working-tree copy would have (1) SILENTLY DROPPED ten committed rows from the record - including the other lane's measured defects (the CLI path never selecting, the plugin home, the no-contrast finding, the 0.11.0 arm policy) - because git add stages the FILE and the file was older than the commit, and (2) REWRITTEN one committed row with the $ref direction stated backwards, the one thing the ledger's law forbids - the rewound file's mtime coincides with this lane's day file and roadmap, whose content is identical to HEAD, so a bulk write left reports/LOG.md holding a stale copy; no stash exists (git stash list is empty) and the reflog shows only ordinary commits, so the rewinding step leaves no trace in this repo's history and only the EFFECT is recorded here - WHAT WAS DONE: the working tree was restored to the committed bytes (git checkout HEAD -- reports/LOG.md) - ten rows added back, the reversed row restored - and nothing of the working tree's own content was lost, since the only worktree-only line was the corrupted variant of an existing row - THE STANDING RULE THIS ADDS, for every lane that appends to the LOG: (1) append to the working tree AND commit a blob anchored at HEAD, and the two must agree (the recipe: git show HEAD:reports/LOG.md > f; cat row >> f; git update-index --cacheinfo 100644,$(git hash-object -w f),reports/LOG.md, committed through a temporary index so the shared index is never touched); (2) AFTER the commit, re-sync the working tree with git checkout HEAD -- reports/LOG.md, because a commit made from a temp index does NOT update the working tree - that stale-copy default is how the ten rows went missing in the first place; (3) BEFORE staging the LOG, diff <(git show HEAD:reports/LOG.md) reports/LOG.md must show only ADDED rows - deletions or a changed row mean the working tree is stale, so restore it before staging; (4) tools/check-handoff.sh does not cover this (it checks the kit's own files, not the LOG's freshness against HEAD), so the check is manual and belongs to whoever stages - kit: card + LOG row + a day-file line + the lane prompt's addendum; no other lane's file edited, no spec/schema/corpus/vector/umpire byte changed, nothing pushed |
| RR-W8BARMSTATE-20261111 | w8b-armstate | PLUGIN 0.11.0 RESOLVES THE ARM PER SESSION BUT RECORDS IT PER PROCESS - THE SIDECAR CAN ATTEST ANOTHER SESSION'S ARM, AND THE COUNTERS ARE CUMULATIVE, NOT THE SESSION'S - their 3455f01 ("plugin 0.11.0: the arm is resolved per session, not per process") is the right DESIGN and is confirmed correct at its committed bytes: packages/hermes/live/profile_policy.py resolves the arm per CALL from hermes_constants.get_hermes_home() (the gateway's per-turn contextvar: gateway/run.py::_profile_runtime_scope -> set_hermes_home_override -> copy_context) and reads exactly two keys from that home's config.yaml (context.engine and plugins.entries.ctxvm-tracer.settings.budgetTokens) with a narrow scanner (no PyYAML in the gateway interpreter); the engine now declines to select when the session's profile says compressor (context_engine.py: _meter["skipped_control"] += 1, request untouched) and adopts the session's budget only when its constructor was given none and the home exists, and the plugin registers with v_budget=None (__init__.py:1839-1841); the sidecar gains arm, engineSetting and skippedControl and profile is now the session's home (__init__.py:612-626) - this IS the analogue the books name: the profile carries the arm's settings, resolved per invocation rather than snapshotted once per process (handoff/packets/P8-cache-stable-selection.md); their measurement: policy(w8bn-naive) -> control/compressor/200000/default, policy(w8bn-paged) -> paged/ctxvm/70000/settings - THE CATCH, from the same committed blob: _state() is the tracer's PROCESS-GLOBAL state (a dict on sys, so it survives re-imports; __init__.py:680-694); the resolved arm is written into it at hook time (__init__.py:1148-1152: serving_profile, budget_tokens, budget_source, arm, engine_setting) and read back at FLUSH time, i.e. later, at session end (__init__.py:612-626) - so (1) CROSS-SESSION OVERWRITE: between an arm session's hook and its flush any other session served by the same gateway process overwrites those five fields, and the operator's own chat is served by that same gateway (their own record: "the newest sidecar heuristic was reading the operator's own chat", RR-W8B-CONTRAST-20261111) with the default profile's plugin enabled - the window between an arm session's last hook and its flush is exactly when the operator's chat can replace arm, profile, engineSetting and budgetTokens with the default profile's values, so the sidecar attests ANOTHER SESSION'S ARM: the profile "default" defect, back in the record rather than in the settings; (2) CUMULATIVE COUNTERS: engineSelections and skippedControl are read from the engine instance's meter (__init__.py:503-514 and 529-536) and that instance lives in the same process-global state (:1841), so the meter counts EVERY selection or control-decline that instance ever made - engineSelections > 0 in a sidecar is not evidence about THAT session, and skippedControl is incremented by the decline path, which the operator's default-profile chat triggers on every turn (its profile says compressor), so a PAGED arm's sidecar can show skippedControl > 0 from another session's turns; (3) CROSS-ARM CONTAMINATION: state["engine_obj"] is overwritten by whichever profile registered its engine last, so the counters a sidecar reports can come from the OTHER arm's engine instance - their own code already states the rule for the engine field ("meaningful only when one gateway serves one arm (plan 4c); a multiplexer serving both cannot attest either, and the runner must enforce the single-arm gateway", __init__.py:612-615), which does not cover the counters - so the commit title is accurate about the RESOLUTION and silent about the RECORDING, and the two are different claims - THREE CHANGES, CHEAPEST FIRST: (1) KEY THE ARM FIELDS BY SESSION ID - the hook already has session_id in its kwargs (_stamp_environment reads kwargs.get("session_id")), the flush path already has one (_coverage_manifest(session_id, ...)), and the file already has the per-session-dict precedent (state.setdefault("flushed_at", {})[session_id] = time.time(), :1100-1102), so one dict (st.setdefault("arms", {})[session_id] = {...}) and the flush reads st["arms"].get(session_id) - nothing else changes and the fallback path stays; | RR-W8CSCHEMA-20261111 | w8c-schema | THE IN-FLIGHT FIX IS SCHEMA-SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION: IT WRITES SHAPES RFC-0 ALREADY HAS, AND THE GATE FOR IT IS THE SEALED v0 VALIDATOR - verified at the sealed schema (spec/rfc-0/x-cabi.schema-v0.json): page.evict IS AN EXISTING v0 VERB (:73-84) with "required": ["type", "turn", "page", "stubText"] and "additionalProperties": false - so the emitted row must carry exactly the verb, the turn, the page identity and the STUB TEXT (what the model sees in place of the page), and NOTHING ELSE: no extra field, no telemetry on the row; the codec already has the variant (Event::PageEvict, model.rs:192) - and turn.render.evictedTokens IS AN EXISTING v0 REQUIRED FIELD (:119, integer >= 0 at :124), so the dropped total can be reported there without touching the schema - so NO RFC-0.1 CARD, NO VALIDATOR CHANGE AND NO VERSION GATE are needed for the other lane's fix 1 (engine -> observer reporting): law 1 stays intact - THE GATE FOR THE FIX, WHEN IT LANDS (one command, the same validator the CI runs): python3 spec/rfc-0/validate_schema.py <the new capture> - an evict row with a missing stubText, or with one field too many, fails the sealed v0 schema, and that is worth saying explicitly because the fix's whole purpose is to make the TRACE witness the policy: a trace that does not validate is not a witness - AND A SECOND POINT ON THE SAME THEME: the arm-aware verifier (fix 2) is a RUNNER-side change, and the arm it should read is the RUNNER'S OWN record of which profile it invoked - the capture's plugin.arm is NOT a safe source for it, because that field is a flush-time global read (reports/RR-W8CCELL01-20261111.md section 4: the paged arm's own capture attests the control's budget); reading the runner's own invocation is what keeps the check correct - kit: a card append + LOG row + a day-file line; no file of the other lane's was edited, nothing pushed |
| W8C-REPORTING-20261111 | W8C / plugin **0.13.0** | **THE ENGINE NOW TELLS THE OBSERVER WHAT IT DROPPED** — the missing link that made cell-01 NO-OP. `select_context` computes `evictedTokens` (sum of the dropped units) and the dropped pages' identity tokens, and calls an observer the plugin installs per session in `pre_llm_call` (`engine.set_observer`, so one engine serving many arms still lands in the right tracer); the tracer accumulates `note_evicted()` and writes it into the NEXT `turn.render.evictedTokens`, which had been hard-coded to 0 since the tracer was written. **No `page.evict` event is emitted and that is deliberate**: this policy REMOVES dropped messages rather than substituting stubs, and RFC-0's `page.evict` requires a stub — writing one would be a lie about the wire, so the count belongs to `turn.render` and the detail to the sidecar. Tests: the observer receives a positive count and names the dropped pages under pressure; an unchanged request reports nothing (L7); the tracer lands an eviction on the turn it was reported in. Gate **ALL GATES GREEN**, python 200; installed 0.13.0 (four files). Re-run of `w8c-cell-01` now depends only on a gateway restart |
| RR-W8CEVICT-20261111 | w8c-evict | THE OTHER LANE'S "NO page.evict" DECISION IS SOUND, AND THE REVIEW SIDE ADDS WHAT THE SIDECAR'S EVICTION DETAIL MUST SATISFY TO BE CHECKABLE AGAINST THE TRACE - the other lane's agent commented on this lane's card: "the card asks for page.evict and I did not emit it: this policy removes the dropped messages rather than substituting stubs, and RFC-0's page.evict requires a stub. Emitting one would describe a wire we did not send. So the count belongs to turn.render.evictedTokens and the detail to the sidecar; page.evict stays for policies that actually stub. That is an instrument-honesty decision, not an omission - and it means the verifier's evictions > 0 gate is now satisfiable by the trace without a fabricated event" - THIS LANE'S VERDICT: THE DECISION IS CORRECT, AND IT IS VERIFIED IN THE CODE AND IN THE SCHEMA - VERIFIED, THIS POLICY DROPS, IT DOES NOT STUB: the hermes engine's selection path (packages/hermes/live/context_engine.py:366-444) is _classify -> _update_page_state -> _credit_demand -> _hard_keep -> _plan -> _assemble(..., dropped, repositioned), and the returned list is built from what survives; there is NO STUB SUBSTITUTION anywhere in this engine (no stub text is produced for a dropped page) because the rust engine's stub mechanism (ctxvm_core::render's Slot { mode, stub_text, ... }) belongs to the ctxvm core, not to this host-side engine - so the hermes policy's wire shape is "the message is gone", not "the message was replaced by a stub" - VERIFIED, RFC-0's page.evict IS THE STUB EVENT: spec/rfc-0/x-cabi.schema-v0.json:73-84 gives "page.evict" with additionalProperties false and required ["type","turn","page","stubText"], and stubText is THE REPLACEMENT THE MODEL SAW; a drop-policy has no such replacement, so a page.evict row here would assert a substitution that never happened - the row's own shape would be the falsehood - and their phrasing is exactly right, "emitting one would describe a wire we did not send" - AND THE SHAPE THAT FITS IS ALREADY IN RFC-0: turn.render.evictedTokens is a REQUIRED field ("the render set for that call: resident/evicted token totals + headroom", ctxvm-codec/src/model.rs:231-235) and does not presuppose substitution, so the count belongs there, the per-page detail belongs in the SIDECAR (not a trace; governed by tools/coverage_check.py), and page.evict stays for the policies that really stub (the ctxvm core's own page-table policies) - THIS LANE'S OWN EARLIER NOTE IS HEREBY CORRECTED IN THAT RESPECT: the card's section 6 asked for "page.evict per dropped page", and that ask was WRONG FOR THIS ENGINE; the correction is an erratum on the REQUEST, not on the decision, which is better than the request was - THE RULE THIS DECISION ESTABLISHES, WORTH WRITING DOWN BECAUSE IT WILL RECUR: A MISSING EVENT IS A GAP, A WRONG EVENT IS A FALSEHOOD - an instrument that emits an event whose shape asserts something the policy did not do has made the trace lie in a way no checker can catch (the shape is valid, the claim is false), so where the wire shape does not match the event's semantics the honest move is to report the COUNT in the field that can carry it and the DETAIL outside the trace - WHAT THE SIDECAR'S EVICTION DETAIL MUST SATISFY TO BE CHECKABLE, THE REVIEW SIDE'S ADDITION, ON THE proseMentions PRECEDENT (reports/RR-W8CVERIFY-20261111.md section 2) AND THE CHECKER THAT GOVERNS IT: (1) EVERY DROPPED PAGE MUST NAME A PAGE THE TRACE DECLARED, keyed by the logicalRef, the same string the trace's page.declare carries and the same key form proseMentions uses (the key invariant was verified at src/plugin.py:308 and confirmed on a real capture); (2) THE PER-PAGE TOKEN TOTALS MUST SUM TO THE TRACE'S turn.render.evictedTokens for that turn - the sidecar's detail and the trace's count are two independent statements about the same event and the checker is where they must agree, which is the cross-check a drop-policy CAN have and the only witness it will ever get; (3) A CONTROL ARM MUST REPORT NO EVICTIONS AT ALL (no selections means nothing dropped) - the arm-aware counterpart of the verifier fix; (4) repositioned is a SECOND KIND OF CHANGE and must not be folded into the eviction count (the engine's payload carries both, and only the dropped units' tokens belong in evictedTokens) - ONE IMPLEMENTATION NOTE FROM READING THE IN-FLIGHT PAYLOAD: the engine reports "dropped": [sorted(units[i].id_toks) ...] - the page's IDENTIFIER TOKENS, not its logicalRef; the observer can map tokens to ref (it holds _declared keyed by ref with the pages' identities) but a token list is ambiguous where two pages share argument tokens and the checker's rule needs the precise key, so carrying the logicalRef in the engine -> observer payload (or resolving it unambiguously) makes rule 1 checkable as stated - WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE CARD, THE BLOCK AND THE NEXT PAIR: the VERIFIER'S evictions > 0 GATE IS NOW SATISFIABLE BY THE TRACE without a fabricated event, which is what makes the next pair bookable at all; the block's prediction ("evictions > 0") is therefore checkable against the trace, and the ARM-AWARE VERIFIER fix is still needed alongside it (a correct control must not be labelled VOID); the CLAIM SET does not change - still no cell until a run's verifier is satisfied, the request series and the provider receipts remain the treatment's evidence, and NO SAVING MAY BE CLAIMED (law 7: the paged arm's uncached input was 4.77x the control's and its cache_read 0.64x, so fewer tokens were bought with cache misses on both sides of the cache) - kit: card + LOG row + a day-file line + the roadmap's W8C pointer; no file of the other lane's was edited, no spec/schema/corpus/vector/umpire byte changed, nothing pushed |
